#                                           the wreck page and waits. A bike with no player record at
#                                           +0x20 returns before any of it. Callers pass kind 0 (placed
#                                           again: lap, bounds, restart) or 1 (after a wreck)
player_hand_reset           boot 0x0027bef0 (bike, pad) slot +0xe8: the pad's reset chord. Gated on no
#                                           AI route, +0x14d0 clear, the mode, and the reason not being
#                                           13, then two pad bindings (0x4c, 0x4d) and the owner's pad
#                                           index; takes +0x14d0 and asks for kind 1 with no page.
#                                           An event handler: fires on the press event of either
#                                           command while the other is held (input manager slot
#                                           +0x1c). Shipped table: 0x4c = L1, 0x4d = R1 (P20)
binding_is_event            boot 0x0011ce10 (command, event kinds, event, ...) does this pad event's
#                                           control match the command's first or second binding
binding_first               boot 0x0011d080 (map, command, out, pad) map+0x138 + pad*0x138 + cmd*4
binding_second              boot 0x0011d0e0 (map, command, out, pad) map+0x3a8 + pad*0x138 + cmd*4.
#                                           The map is *(app_ptr)+0x104 (the input manager) +0x648;
#                                           0x4e commands, a control index each (the port's
#                                           input::Control order), -1 unbound. recording2's state:
#                                           0x11-0x13 Circle/Triangle/R1 (the trick buttons), 0x4c L1,
#                                           0x4d R1, no second bindings; command 4 Start (pause)
jump_record_end             boot 0x0027b6e0 (bike) ends the player's current jump record; called by both
#                                           the wreck entry and the reset request before anything else
wreck_page_raise            boot 0x001c84b0 (page handle, which screen, node) pushes the wreck page onto
#                                           a screen's page list
wreck_page_show             boot 0x001ffe80 (screen, which) the second half of raising it
wreck_page_query            boot 0x001c8450 (page handle, which screen) -> group[which]->+0x54, one of
#                                           the two things the machine asks before raising a page. The
#                                           groups are an array of pointers at handle+0x40
wreck_page_query_back       boot 0x001c8480 (page handle, which screen) -> group[which]->+0x48, the
#                                           other one
network_session             boot 0x004e2778 the network session pointer; null offline, and every notify
#                                           in this area is gated on it
trick_queue_clear           boot 0x0025a4b0 (list) empties the rider's trick queue, freeing each node
orientation_transform_point boot 0x001b3060 (out, orientation, point) a VU0 transform; the first call
#                                           the base wreck entry makes after its three state writes,
#                                           which is where a proof of those writes can stop
camera_wreck_cut            boot 0x003dcb90 (camera, 0) what the player's wreck entry tells the camera
screen_wreck_notice         boot 0x001ffcb0 (screen, reason) what it tells the front end

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S2 — actor lifetime: the rest of the manager R6 found, the record's own constructor, and the two
# global name registries the "kind" and "is-a" bits come out of. How each was read is in
# research/journal/2026-10-15-s2-s2-actors. The manager's object: +0x30 master list, +0x3c group list,
# both {count, head, tail}; a list node is {item, prev, next}. An actor record: +0x00 kind bit, +0x04
# class mask, +0x0c the constructor's third argument, +0x10 the word both searches gate on, +0x18 scene
# node, +0x1c manager, +0x20 player, +0x24/+0x30/+0x3c three sub-lists, +0x48 vtable.
#
# The manager's vtable (0x00452ae0) past the base class: +0x74 Add, +0x78 Remove, +0x7c and +0x8c the
# two per-frame dispatch passes, +0x80 nearest, +0x84 nearest-of-kinds, +0x88 the two-actor exchange.
actor_manager_remove        boot 0x0021a9d0 Remove(actor): find in the master list, unlink it there,
#                                           clear actor+0x1c, then unlink it from the group whose head
#                                           member's kind equals the actor's. Leaves an emptied group
#                                           in the list, which the next lookup reads through
actor_manager_each_early    boot 0x0021aa80 walk the master list head->tail calling vtable +0x28 on
#                                           each actor
actor_manager_each_late     boot 0x0021aef0 the same walk calling vtable +0x58
actor_manager_nearest       boot 0x0021aad0 (manager, point*, out_f32|0, exclude) -> actor|0: master
#                                           list order; gates on actor+0x18 then +0x10; distance from
#                                           node_box_centre through vsub.xyz/mula.s/madda.s/madd.s/
#                                           sqrt.s; keeps strictly nearer (c.olt.s) and rejects
#                                           <= 1e-6 (c.ole.s, 0x358637bd); out gets FLT_MAX when
#                                           nothing matched
actor_manager_nearest_kinds boot 0x0021ac00 (manager, mask_ptr, point*, out_f32|0, exclude) -> actor|0:
#                                           the same, with actor+0x00 & *mask_ptr tested first. The
#                                           mask is read through a pointer, once per actor
actor_pair_exchange         boot 0x0021ad50 (manager, a, b): not a list walk — walks a's sub-list
#                                           through its own vtable (+0x2c count, +0x30 at, +0x34 take)
#                                           offering each entry to b (+0x40 accept, +0x44 notify), then
#                                           the same with a and b swapped
actor_list_ctor             boot 0x0021afc0 zero the count, head and tail. 0x0021af40 is the group
#                                           list's instantiation of the same
actor_list_count            boot 0x0021af60 the count word
actor_list_at               boot 0x0021af70 (list, i) -> item i steps from the head, or 0 past the end;
#                                           i <= 0 gives the head
actor_list_remove           boot 0x0021b020 (list, item) -> item|0: find, then unlink
actor_list_unlink           boot 0x0021b080 (list, node): mend head/tail/prev/next, operator delete the
#                                           node, count -= 1
actor_list_clear            boot 0x0021b1d0 zero each node's item word, free the nodes head first,
#                                           then zero the three words. 0x0021b370 is the group list's
actor_list_dtor             boot 0x0021b230 (list, free_object): clear, and operator delete the list
#                                           object itself when the second argument is > 0 — which is
#                                           how a group object goes. 0x0021b3d0 is the group list's
group_list_append           boot 0x0021b2c0 list_append for the group list (R6's note calls this the
#                                           "second half" of 0x0021b120; they are two instantiations of
#                                           one template, not two halves)
actor_record_ctor           boot 0x00245120 (record, type_name, arg3): vtable at +0x48, three empty
#                                           sub-lists, +0x00 = name_bit(actor_kind_names, type_name),
#                                           +0x04/+0x08/+0x18/+0x1c/+0x20 = 0, +0x0c = arg3,
#                                           +0x10 = +0x14 = 1. Registers nothing with the manager
actor_record_dtor           boot 0x002451c0 frees the items of the sub-list at +0x24 and then all three
#                                           sub-lists. Does not tell the manager
actor_class_descriptor_ctor boot 0x002450c0 (descriptor, class_name, record_classes_ptr, arg4): one per
#                                           level of an actor's hierarchy. descriptor+0x00 = arg4,
#                                           +0x04 = name_bit(actor_class_names, class_name), and that
#                                           bit is ORed into the record's +0x04
name_bit                    boot 0x00281cb0 (registry, name) -> bit: walk the registry's list from the
#                                           head comparing the first 0x80 bytes, shifting a running 1
#                                           left once per entry walked past; on a miss, 0x80 bytes are
#                                           allocated, strncpy'd and appended, and the shifted value is
#                                           returned. So slot 31 gets 0x80000000 and every slot from 32
#                                           on gets 0
name_registry_clear         boot 0x00281df0 operator delete[] each name in index order, then free the
#                                           nodes
actor_kind_names            boot 0x004e1e88 the type-name registry: {count, head, tail}. An actor's
#                                           kind is its type name's bit in here
actor_class_names           boot 0x004e1e68 the class-name registry, same shape
node_box_centre             boot 0x001b4560 (node, out_centre*, out_extent*): the scene node's world
#                                           bounding box, refreshed through the node's own vtable if
#                                           stale. Both out quadwords get w = 1.0f. Scene-graph work,
#                                           hooked rather than ported in S2
player_manager_remove_all   boot 0x00245d00 the player manager's teardown: destroys the players it
#                                           holds, which the actor manager never does to actors

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S14 — the freeride opponents, and the end of the tick-exact acceptance window. Measured from the
# recording's save state (4 racers on Free09) and a 300-sample idle capture. What was reasoned rather
# than measured is in research/journal/2026-10-15-s14-s14-freeride-opponent.
freeride_racer_count        boot 0x002b0a20 the league-2 / activity-type-5 (freeride) start: sets the
#                                           activity type to 5, then NumberOfRacers = 4 when the
#                                           player count is 1, else the player count, and seeds srand
#                                           from the wall clock. So a one-player freeride is always
#                                           four racers, with no setting involved
spawn_racers                boot 0x00207b20 spawns the humans first, then NumberOfRacers - humans AI
#                                           riders with rand()-chosen identities, and takes the AI
#                                           skill range from the event's +0x117 (90, the MXEvent
#                                           constructor default, maps to [4, 4]). Humans first is why
#                                           the player manager's list head is the human
ai_update_mx                boot 0x00386d30 MXVehicleAI.cpp per-tick update, and
ai_update_vehicle           boot 0x002858d0 VehicleAI.cpp's: both build the look-ahead feeler fan and
#                                           run the per-rider avoidance scan when
#                                           (flags&0x80) == 0 || (flags&0x40) == 0 — i.e. the probing
#                                           is skipped only when BOTH bits are set, so Free10/Free06
#                                           (0x40 set, hit_freeride_attach above) are the only areas
#                                           that suppress it. A third test, (flags&0xc0) == 0, admits
#                                           the type-3 scenery objects
ai_tick_flag                boot 0x00383580 sets and clears the per-tick bit 0x80 of the same flags
vehicle_wreck               boot 0x00252ca0 Wreck(reason), vtable slot +0xfc: sets vehicle+0xb7c = 1
#                                           and hands the body to the ragdoll subsystem at +0x7a8,
#                                           which is integrating Wreckbike.phy from the build. The
#                                           bike's own parameter pointers (+0x7a0, +0x7a4) are NOT
#                                           rewritten. On reason 0xd it draws three rand() into the
#                                           ragdoll's +0x1b0/+0x1b4/+0x1b8; the wreck test at
#                                           0x002522c0 draws two more (0x00252534, 0x00252568)
vehicle_wreck_name          boot 0x0024fca0 (from 0x0027aa70) snprintfs "%sWreck%s.phy" into
#                                           vehicle+0x188 at build time, 0x104 bytes, "Bike\\" from
#                                           0x004e2000 — "Bike\Wreckbike.phy". The .phy loader
#                                           0x002539e0 opens it at the end of the build and passes it
#                                           to *(vehicle+0x7a8)'s vtable slot 0xe8
opponent_difficulty_widget  boot 0x002ccd10 the front-end screen loader that binds widget names,
#                                           "OpponentDifficulty" among them. OPPONENT_ENABLED and
#                                           OPPONENT_DIFFICULTY are front-end identifiers, not .cfg
#                                           keys: nothing on the simulation side reads either name
#
# Object links, from the save state (every one of the five players resolved the same way):
#   player+0x34       kind: 1 human, 2 AI
#   player+0x48 -> +0x0   the MotocrossVehicle (actor type 2): +0x84 this bike's physics name
#                         (P for the human, A for the AI riders), +0x188 "Bike\Wreckbike.phy" for all
#                         of them, +0xb7c the wrecked flag
#   player+0x4c -> +0x0   the type-4 bike actor the acceptance capture watches (+0x130 velocity,
#                         +0x180 transform)
# The human is the player manager's list head, so @game_manager -> 0x30 -> 0x34 -> 0x0 is the human
# player and reaches its bike without counting actor-list hops. watch/acceptance.toml's five -> 0x8
# hops land on actor index 5, which in this session is an AI rider; the human is index 8.
#
# With the state loaded and nothing pressed, rand_state still advances 2..19+ draws per simulation
# tick (median ~8) — the opponents and the effects. A port without them cannot match the LCG past
# tick 0, which is where the tick-exact window has to end: at the first wreck or contact.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S7 — the camera for normal play. The port is crates/game-sim/src/camera.rs and the proofs are
# crates/game-sim/tests/camera.rs. How each one was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-15-s7-s7-camera.
#
# One C++ class (its __FILE__ is "SimpleFollowCam.cpp", 0x0042d350), reached through the secondary-base
# vtable at 0x00452060. The object's fields: +0x7c viewport (its +0x19c is the collision-derived
# nearest distance), +0x8c target actor (+0x10 body point, +0x30 forward row, +0x40 second basis row,
# +0x50 velocity, +0x84 lean), +0xa0..0xac the offset copy, +0xd0 distance, +0xd4 yaw, +0xd8 pitch,
# +0xdc base fov, +0xe0/+0xe4 camera/look swing, +0xe8 collision tester, +0xf0..+0x104 the six vector
# filter *pointers*, +0x114/+0x118 the fov and spare filter pointers, +0x11c..+0x128 the four user
# offsets (zoom, yaw, pitch, fov), +0x13c/+0x140/+0x144 the three reverse ramps, +0x148 reverse speed
# threshold, +0x14c speed reference (ctor: 1.0), +0x150 look-at height bias (ctor: 3.0), +0x158/+0x15c
# current/previous preset, +0x160 preset count, +0x164 preset array, +0x168 snap, +0x16c pitch-down
# lock, +0x170 collision-hit flag, +0x174 viewport index. A filter is 5 floats: value, tau, alpha,
# +FLT_MAX, -FLT_MAX.
camera_update               boot 0x001fa4c0 (dt in f12, cam) the whole frame, vtable slot 0x98. Writes
#                                           only its own fields and the viewport; calls no rand and
#                                           does not touch the frame clock, which is why the port is
#                                           allowed to be presentation
camera_get_fov              boot 0x001fb0b0 (cam) -> f0: base + user fov, clamped to the preset's
#                                           MinFOV/MaxFOV with the user offset walked back to the stop
camera_forward_reverse      boot 0x001fb120 (speed in f12, cam, heading, vel) -> 0/1: acos of
#                                           normalize(vel) . heading through the shared table at
#                                           acos_table_ptr; past a quarter turn and above cam+0x148 it
#                                           ramps +0x13c/+0x140/+0x144 up to the preset's three
camera_aim                  boot 0x001fb440 (swing in f12, cam): yaw = AngleY - user yaw - swing*
#                                           CameraSwing through fmodf, pitch = user + AngleX - reverse
#                                           ramp clamped to +-1.4835324 with the user offset rewritten
camera_look_at              boot 0x001fb600 (speed, fov ratio, swing in f12..f14, out, cam, target
#                                           point, heading, &vel, reverse) the look-ahead point
camera_position             boot 0x001fba30 (out, cam, dir, target point, heading, &look at, sideways,
#                                           &vel): out = target point + dir * cam+0xd0. Everything
#                                           else in it — a yaw-driven swing over two 3pi/8 arcs — is
#                                           computed into stack slots nothing reads
camera_filter               boot 0x001fbc20 (dt in f12, cam, position, look at, &fov, &spare, target
#                                           point): the eight one-pole lags, or the snap that loads
#                                           them instead when the preset does not filter
camera_set_preset           boot 0x001fabb0 (cam, index): copies the preset into the object, sets the
#                                           six taus, arms the snap unfiltered -> filtered, and clears
#                                           the four user offsets unless leaving a look-back preset
camera_nudge                boot 0x001fc630 (stick x, stick y in f14/f15, cam, control table, pad): the
#                                           right stick. Reads analogue controls 0x1e and 0x1f through
#                                           pad_control_index; with either held the stick zooms or
#                                           widens, otherwise it swings yaw and pitch
camera_load_presets         boot 0x001fbf50 (cam, file): fills the 0xa0-byte preset records from the
#                                           camera ini. Section [NumberOfPresets], then Preset%d (or
#                                           Preset%dh/v/q by viewport index, or Preset%dw/x/z when the
#                                           word at 0x004211a0 is 3). [R9: not an index — cam+0x174 is
#                                           the split mode: 0 plain, 1 h, 2 v, else q; at 16:9 (0x004211a0
#                                           == 3) 0 and 1 w, 2 x, else z. See the R9 section.] Keys, in record order: +0x00
#                                           LookFilters, +0x10 PosFilters, +0x20 OffsetFromBodyPoint,
#                                           +0x30 Name[32], +0x50 AngleY and +0x54 AngleX (degrees,
#                                           times 0.01745329), +0x58 FollowingDistance, +0x5c FOV,
#                                           +0x60 ReverseAngleXOffset, +0x64 ReverseFollowingOffset,
#                                           +0x68 ReverseLookAheadOffset, +0x6c
#                                           ForwardReverseSwitchSpeed, +0x70 CycleThruThisPreset,
#                                           +0x74 FilterThisPreset, +0x78 LookAheadDistance, +0x7c
#                                           IsLookBackCam, +0x80 CameraSwing, +0x84 LookSwing, +0x88
#                                           MinFollowingDistance, +0x8c MaxFollowingDistance, +0x90
#                                           MinFOV, +0x94 MaxFOV, +0x98 LowSpeedAngleXVelCutoff, +0x9c
#                                           LowSpeedAngleXScaleFactor. A missing key keeps the zero
camera_set_look_filters     boot 0x001fad70 (cam, values or 0, taus or 0) for +0xf0/+0xf4/+0xf8:
#                                           FLT_MAX in a lane means leave that one alone; a tau also
#                                           resets alpha to 1.0
camera_set_pos_filters      boot 0x001faf00 the same for +0xfc/+0x100/+0x104
camera_frame_noop           boot 0x001fb090 vtable slot 0x108 in this class: `return 1`, no transform
camera_ground_probe_zero    boot 0x001fb0a0 vtable slot 0x10c in this class: `return 0.0f`, so the
#                                           look-at's ground lift is always zero
camera_vtable               boot 0x00452060 the secondary-base vtable: +0x98 update, +0x100/+0x104 the
#                                           filter setters, +0x108 frame, +0x10c ground probe, +0x110
#                                           fov, +0x114 forward/reverse, +0x118 aim, +0x11c look at,
#                                           +0x120 position, +0x124 filter, +0x128 load presets,
#                                           +0x12c nudge
rotate_about_axis           boot 0x001344e0 (angle in f12, unit axis, v, out): Rodrigues through the
#                                           6000-entry curve table — wrap into +-pi twice, index by
#                                           (angle+pi)*curve_table_invstep, correct the entry with the
#                                           leftover angle's derivative, then d*axis + cos*perp +
#                                           sin*cross(axis, perp). out.w = 1.0
pad_control_index           boot 0x0011e2d0 (pad, control, 2, 10, ...) -> index into a 0x18-stride
#                                           control table; the value is at +4 of the entry
# The curve table's builder reaches the SDK's double-precision cos and sin — already named above as
# cos_double and sin_double — through float_to_double (0x00417b48) and double_to_float (0x00418700).
camera_park_nudges          boot 0x001fa440 (cam) vtable slot 0xf0: copies +0x11c..+0x128, the player's
#                                           four nudges, into the shadow at +0x12c..+0x138
camera_unpark_nudges        boot 0x001fa470 (cam) vtable slot 0xf4: copies them back
camera_next_preset          boot 0x001fa2e0 (cam) vtable slot 0xe8: the camera button. From +0x158
#                                           plus one, wrapping to 0, the first preset whose +0x70
#                                           CycleThruThisPreset is set, through slot 0xfc; with +0x160
#                                           == 1 it sets preset 0. No cycling preset: loops forever
camera_previous_preset      boot 0x001fa3a0 (cam) vtable slot 0xec: the same walk downwards, wrapping
#                                           to +0x160 - 1, with no one-preset case
camera_clear_nudges         boot 0x001fa4a0 (cam) vtable slot 0xf8: zeroes all four. Nothing in the
#                                           camera calls this one
viewport_set_eye            boot 0x00112ac0 (viewport, &position or 0, &dir or 0, &up or 0, &index or
#                                           0, &fov or 0): the viewport's setter the camera's last
#                                           three lines go through. Writes +0x140 position, +0x150
#                                           and +0x160 the two normalised axes, +0x174 index, +0x170
#                                           fov when it is not zero, all only while +0x208 and +0x209
#                                           are both -1
camera_base_update          boot 0x00113ec0 (dt in f12, cam): the base class's own update, which the
#                                           camera's last line calls. Walks the list at cam+0x80 and
#                                           then 0x00133500; nothing of it is the camera

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# V2 — the camera of the frame on screen, which is what a side-by-side screenshot has to be posed from.
# Read out of the human's save state, not decompiled: no writer was found, so these are named from
# their contents. research/journal/2026-10-15-v2-v2-side-by-side.
#
render_camera           gameplay 0x0046a540 the render camera as a 4x4 in the files' row convention:
#                                           rows x/y/z and the eye at +0x30 (0x0046a570), w = 1
view_projection         gameplay 0x0046a580 that frame's view-projection, **relative to the eye** —
#                                           project (p - eye), not p. Columns 0/1/3 give right, up and
#                                           forward; the lengths of columns 0 and 1 are 1/tan(fov/2),
#                                           so this matrix alone yields the field of view (85.00 across,
#                                           68.997 down in the human's state = 4:3 on a 640x448 buffer)
#
# Confirmed by projection rather than by code: through 0x0046a580 the human's bike origin lands on pixel
# (318, 305) of 640x448 and its seat on (318, 220), which is where the frame stored inside the state has
# them. The eye sits 11.1 ft behind and 7.0 above the bike, 13.1 away — cpresets.ini's FollowingDistance
# = 13 and FOV = 85 for the first follow camera (V1), i.e. this is the chase camera after the game's own
# filtering. Another activity (replay, split screen) may keep its camera elsewhere; unchecked.
# V2 ran before S7 landed, so "no writer was found" is V2's search, not a standing fact: the S7 block
# above decompiles the follow camera whose filtered output this is, and its last three lines go through
# viewport_set_eye (0x00112ac0) into a *viewport* object's +0x140/+0x150/+0x160/+0x170. Whether that
# viewport is the global at 0x0046a540 was checked by neither task.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S3 — movement on the ground. The one step the bike's update runs while a wheel is down, the five
# pieces of it that are small enough to port on their own, and the callees it drives in order. The
# port is crates/game-sim/src/move.rs and the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/move.rs; how each one
# was found is in research/journal/2026-10-15-s3-s3-movement.
bike_ground_step boot 0x00257a00 (dt in f12, bike) -> the whole ground frame: the pose (centre of
#                                gravity, steering, chassis flags), the donut scale, both spring
#                                rates, the gearbox, the clutch, and then (int)(dt*55)+1 substeps of
#                                gravity, drag, the reverse push and twenty-one calls each
bike_clutch boot 0x002572f0 (dt in f12, bike) -> the clutch's launch ramp: engine+0x88's torque
#                           multiplier and the centre-of-gravity travel that goes with it
bike_set_gear boot 0x00246f70 (engine, gear) -> engine+0x68/+0x80/+0x84: the gear, its overall ratio
#                             (final drive * the gear's own) and the reciprocal. Out of 1..gears it
#                             does nothing at all
engine_rev_limit boot 0x00247260 (engine) -> with the clutch locked, slows the driven wheel back to
#                                the rev limit. Runs after the wheel has been integrated
wheel_clear boot 0x0025b200 (wheel) -> empties the eight scalars and three quadwords the suspension
#                           and the tire forces fill in, at the top of every substep
chassis_set_flags boot 0x0015c3a0 (chassis, a, b, c) -> chassis+0x2ac/+0x2b0/+0x2b4
# The callees the ground step drives and S3 does not port: the proof hooks all of these, records the
# order and the dt each one is handed, and compares that with the list the port returns.
bike_stand_upright boot 0x002574e0 (dt in f12, bike) the low-speed upright controller
bike_balance boot 0x002561f0 (dt in f12, bike) the balance-on-one-wheel controller
bike_roll_pitch boot 0x00256470 (dt in f12, bike) the roll and pitch control: the rider's lean
bike_wheel_contacts boot 0x00257820 (dt in f12, bike) distributes the frame's collision contacts onto
#                                   the two wheels. Handed the frame's dt, not the substep's
wheel_suspension boot 0x0025b280 (dt in f12, wheel) the suspension ray and its spring force
wheel_tire_forces boot 0x0025bc70 (dt in f12, wheel) the tire's drive, brake and side forces
wheel_integrate boot 0x0025bbf0 (dt in f12, wheel) the wheel's own spin and steering integration
bike_no_hander boot 0x00255d20 (dt in f12, bike) the no-hander, after its four accumulators are zeroed
engine_update boot 0x00246fd0 (dt in f12, engine) the torque curve, the clutch's slip and the torque
#                             that reaches the driven wheel. Ported and proved by S15; the S15 block
#                             below has the detail, and corrects S3's note that it reads f20 unset
transform_rotate boot 0x001b2ff0 (out, transform, v) -> out.xyz = the transform's three rows times
#                                v's three lanes; the reverse push's only use of the chassis' frame
transform_unrotate boot 0x001b30e0 (out, transform, v) -> the same through the transpose, which is
#                                  how the measured acceleration comes back into the bike's frame
actor_search_reset boot 0x00119be0 (search) empties the nearby-actor search the step refills
actor_search_gather boot 0x00218b80 (list, search, radius) refills it, with a radius of 3
bike_phy_load boot 0x002539e0 (bike, file) the bike's .phy tuning file: every parameter in
#                             crates/game-sim/src/move.rs's Params comes from here, and the journal
#                             entry has the full key -> offset map

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S15 — the engine's torque curve. engine_update's own arithmetic, the two curve helpers behind it and
# the setters that fill the engine in. The port is crates/game-sim/src/move.rs (engine_update,
# curve_seek, curve_value) and the proof is the_engine_is_the_originals in
# crates/game-sim/tests/move.rs; how each one was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-15-s15-engine-torque. engine_update itself is in the S3 block above.
curve_seek boot 0x0013e6f0 (cursor, x in f12) walks a {count, index, frac, xs*} cursor to x: up while
#                          x >= xs[index], then down while x < xs[index-1], then frac =
#                          (xs[index] - x) / (xs[index] - xs[index-1]), or 0 on the bottom point. Off
#                          the top it parks on the last point with frac 0. Both bounds tests unsigned
curve_value boot 0x0013e7c0 (cursor, ys) -> ys[index] - frac * (ys[index] - ys[index-1]), or ys[0] on
#                           the bottom point. Takes the table as an argument, so one seek can read
#                           any number of curves at the same place
wheel_add_drive_torque boot 0x0025c380 (wheel, torque in f12) -> wheel+0x210 += torque. An
#                                      accumulator: the engine, the brake and the tires all add here
engine_set_power_curve boot 0x00246ce0 (engine, n, rpm[], hp[]) fills engine+0x6c/+0x70/+0x74 and the
#                                      +0x8c cursor, turns each hp into a torque (5252*hp/rpm, and a
#                                      point with rpm <= 0 is skipped, not zeroed), keeps the peak and
#                                      its rpm at +0xb8/+0xb0, and sets the rev limit at +0xb4 from
#                                      the last point's rpm
engine_set_gearbox boot 0x00246e80 (engine, n, ratios[], final) -> engine+0x78/+0x7c/+0x40
engine_set_inertia boot 0x00246f20 (engine, inertia in f12) -> engine+0x44, and 1/inertia at +0x48
#                                  when inertia > 0
engine_set_driven_wheel boot 0x00246cd0 (engine, wheel) -> engine+0x9c
engine_ctor boot 0x00246b70 zeroes the engine and sets +0x40/+0x5c/+0x80/+0x84/+0x88/+0xbc to 1.0 and
#                          the gear at +0x68 to 1. +0x30/+0x34 are left null; the bike's own setup at
#                          0x002535c4 points them at bike+0x658 and bike+0x65c, the rate-limited
#                          throttle and clutch (and the two wheels' +0x30 at bike+0x650/+0x654)
# --- S16: the wheel ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The three the ground step drives the wheel through are named above. What they reach, and where the
# ten fields wheel_clear empties are written. Found in research/journal/2026-10-15-s16-*.
ground_query boot 0x001dc960 (world, point in/out, normal out, flags, record) -> moves the point onto
#                            the surface under it and writes that surface's normal. Eighty-four
#                            callers across the game: the world's query, not the wheel's
#                            S64: on the terrain its height and normal are contact_search::ground's
#                            triangle split (not Ground::at's bilinear); the save-state run's wheel
#                            lanes (load point, normal, compression) agree with it to tick 22.
#                            Air state on the rider, bike_air_state 0x00251d20: +0x574 on_ground,
#                            +0x578 both_wheels, +0x57c airborne, +0x580 was_airborne, +0x4f0
#                            hang_time, +0x430 contact_vel, +0x600 crossup
wheel_set_contact boot 0x0025b250 (wheel, owner, vehicle) -> wheel+0xd4 = 1 with the two objects the
#                                 contact belongs to at +0x1f0 and +0x1f4
wheel_surface_drag boot 0x0025c390 (wheel; dt is still in f12 but is not read) -> the rolling
#                                  resistance of the surface under the wheel: the three per-surface
#                                  tables at +0x778, +0x7b8 and +0x7f8, indexed by the contact
#                                  record's surface byte at +0x110, into a force on the chassis and a
#                                  torque at +0x210
wheel_spin_integrate boot 0x0025c530 (dt in f12, wheel) -> the wheel's own spin, and the steering
#                                    hinge's contribution to the chassis' angular velocity. Reads the
#                                    hinge axis at +0x8c0 and its frame at +0x8d0..+0x8f0
wheel_steer_integrate boot 0x0025d1b0 (dt in f12, wheel) -> the steering hinge: +0x6c's torque less
#                                     +0x70's damping, through +0x78's inverse inertia into +0x8b0's
#                                     rate, into +0x8ac's angle, clamped to +0x80..+0x7c with the rate
#                                     killed at either end. +0x6c is spent to zero
wheel_steer_stop boot 0x0025d1a0 (wheel) -> +0x8b0 = 0 and +0x6c = 0: the steered-but-not-balancing
#                                case, where the angle the ground step commanded stands
wheel_contact_velocity boot 0x0025b710 (wheel) -> how fast the contact is moving relative to whatever
#                                     the wheel is touching: +0x170 = the contact point's velocity
#                                     less the other body's at that point (+0x1c0), +0x180 = the
#                                     chassis' velocity (owner+0x260) less the other body's (+0x1b0),
#                                     +0x190 = that in the chassis' frame, and +0x1a0 = 1 so the whole
#                                     thing runs once a substep
wheel_hinge_force boot 0x0025cf40 (scale in f12, wheel, force, point) -> puts a force at a point
#                                through the steering hinge instead of straight into the chassis.
#                                wheel_tire_forces calls it with a scale of exactly 1.0
transform_unpoint boot 0x001b3180 (out, transform, p) -> a world point back into the transform's own
#                                 frame: the transpose of the rotation applied to p less the origin.
#                                 transform_rotate's and transform_unrotate's sibling, and like them
#                                 it may rebuild a lazy chain of parent frames first (0x001b1750)
transform_sync boot 0x001b2770 (transform, flags) -> brings the transform up to date; called with
#                              flags 0 and its answer thrown away
# The five one-line VU0 helpers wheel_tire_forces calls, all inlined in the port (moving words about
# and a masked lane op, nothing that can round differently):
#   0x00107fa0 (out, a) out = a, one quadword     0x00107fb0 vec3_sub_vu, above
#   0x00155c30 (a, b) a.xyz += b.xyz in place     0x0010b290 (out, a, b) out.xyz = a x b, out.w = a.w
#   0x001bb910 (out, a, b) out.xyz = a.xyz * b.xyz, out.w = a.w
# The three constant quadwords it reads. The proof reads the third one out of the image rather than
# trusting the port's copy, so its value is proved and not assumed:
#   0x00426d70 (0, 0, 0, 1)   0x00426d80 (1, 1, 1, 1)   0x00426db0 (0, 1, 0, 1)

# A5 — the whole frame, measured on the live emulator rather than one function at a time. The spec is
# watch/frame-bike.toml, the proofs are the A5 tests at the end of crates/game-sim/tests/replay.rs,
# and how each number was found is in research/journal/2026-10-16-a5-whole-frame.
#
# The riding branch of the ridden actor's per-frame update (0x002520d0, $s0 the actor, $f20 the dt;
# ./kit dis 0x002521a0 0x00252268 lists it), in the order it calls:
#   vt+0x0ec 0x0027ffd0 -> bike_ground_step 0x00257a00, only while +0x748 is 0 -> bike_air_state
#   0x00251d20 -> 0x00133500 (obj+0x50, dt) the attachment walk -> stunt_idle vt+0x124 0x00258cc0 ->
#   stunt_state_pack vt+0x128 0x00258d50 -> stunt_clocks vt+0x130 0x002593e0, whose tail past
#   clock_section_end 0x00259b00 is the animation header -> obj+0xa08 = obj+0xa04 ->
#   ride_state_machine vt+0x0f8 0x0027bc90.
# The dispatch table is obj+0x48 and the twelve slots are read live by research/frame/bike_probe.py,
# so the order above is this save state's own table and not a reading of the listing.
#
# Fields of the ridden actor, measured over 1,400 consecutive ticks of one ride (the human's rider is
# list entry 2 and its chassis entry 8 on the recording's save state; control source 1 on
# +0x20 -> +0x34 tells them from the three AI riders'):
#   +0x600  the crossup angle in radians, written by bike_air_state. The header's airborne arm remaps
#           it onto +0x788 and +0x9e8 in the *same* frame: exact on 513 of 513 airborne ticks, and
#           nothing like it one tick late
#   +0x670  the roll lean, -1..1 -> +0x9e8 = 0.5 + 0.5 * it on a grounded tick, same frame
#   +0x674  the fore-aft lean, -1..1 -> +0x9ec = (it + 1) * 0.5, every tick, same frame
#   +0x6d8  MaxCrossupAngle as the physics holds it, and +0x9e4 as the animation holds it: both
#           0x3f490fd9 = 0.7853980660438538 = 45 degrees, on all 1,400 ticks. This is the retail value
#           S6 could not prove; Params::max_crossup in the port is still handed it by the caller
#   +0x748  the ride state's reason, 0 while riding (S5). Non-zero for 158 of the 1,400 ticks of the
#           ride — two wrecks, reason 11 then 1 — and the reset that follows puts +0x788 back to 0.5
#   +0x820  the seat position the attachment walk writes out of the chassis. Against the chassis'
#           own transform (entry 8, +0x180 row 3) it is a rigid pair within one tick: 0.134 units
#           apart on the same tick against 1.734 one tick late, with 1.853 units of travel in the
#           median tick, so the attachment walk ran after this tick's physics
#   +0x864  the id the state pack latched == +0xa78 the id the header ran on, 1,399 of 1,399 ticks at
#           lag 0 and 1,377 at lag 1: the header latches the pack's id in the pack's own frame
#   +0x8a0  reads as a packed word (0xd4002100, 0xd40c2900) and not the small count the note on
#           stunt_state_pack above suggests. The count the restart test reads is +0xa04, with the
#           previous frame's copy at +0xa08 (both 0 for all 1,400 ticks: never exercised)
#   +0x9f8  the blend rate, 3.0 (0x40400000) throughout, which is the constructor's value

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S19 — the chassis under the bike. The rigid body the ground step pushes on: the integrate virtual
# it ends every substep with, the per-frame speed measure, the transform's rotate/unrotate pair, the
# two-step integrator the body reaches through a pointer on itself, and the contact distributor.
# The port is crates/game-sim/src/chassis.rs, the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/chassis.rs, and
# how each one was found is in research/journal/2026-10-16-s19-s19-chassis. chassis_set_flags,
# bike_wheel_contacts, transform_rotate and transform_unrotate are named in the S3 block above --
# and transform_unrotate's transpose is worth a warning: five MMI packs build it and then a plain
# `move` finishes the third column, which is a 64-bit or and writes only the low half of its
# destination, so that column's z is row 2's own z, still sitting in the register. Read as a
# quadword copy it comes out as row 0's w, which is wrong and looks like a bug in the original.
chassis_integrate boot 0x0015c540 (chassis, dt in f12) the body's own integrate, vtable +0x3c: the
#                                force and the torque the substep accumulated turned into motion,
#                                the transform brought along, and the accumulators emptied. Returns
#                                a constant 1 that no caller reads. Ghidra puts the chassis in
#                                param_2; the prologue (move $16, $4 / mov.s $f20, $f12) says
#                                otherwise
chassis_measure boot 0x0015c4c0 (chassis, dt in f12) the per-frame measure, vtable +0x38: the
#                              orientation as euler angles into +0x90, the velocity's length into
#                              +0x34, then the base class' frame update. Called before the ground
#                              step, so the speed the steering fade reads is last frame's
chassis_base_update boot 0x00133500 (chassis, dt in f12) the base class' per-frame update, which the
#                                  measure tail-calls and which this port does not follow
chassis_clear_contacts boot 0x00158fd0 (chassis) vtable +0xa4: one word, chassis+0x1e0 = 0. The last
#                                     thing every integrate does, on both of its paths
chassis_local_velocity boot 0x0015cbf0 (out, chassis) vtable +0xb4: transform_unrotate of
#                                     chassis+0x260, the velocity in the body's own frame -> +0x270
chassis_world_ang_vel boot 0x0015cbe0 (out, chassis) vtable +0xc0: transform_rotate of chassis+0x290,
#                                    the angular velocity in the world -> +0x280
chassis_vtable boot 0x0044e8f0 the chassis' vtable, so a proof can dispatch through the real one
#                             rather than a fake: +0x38 measure, +0x3c integrate, +0xa4 clear
#                             contacts, +0xb4 local velocity, +0xc0 world angular velocity
integrate_ab2 boot 0x0013e460 (n, x, dx, dt in f12) two-step Adams-Bashforth over n floats:
#                            x[i] += (dt/2) * (3*dx[i] - prev[i]), then prev[i] = dx[i], where prev
#                            is dx + 4*n. Unrolled eight at a time above n = 8 and a plain loop
#                            below; the chassis only ever asks for three or four. The rigid body's
#                            constructor writes this address into chassis+0x25c and the integrate
#                            calls it through that pointer four times a step
quat_to_euler boot 0x0013fa60 (out, q) the orientation as three angles, for the measure's records
quat_from_axis_angle boot 0x0013ec80 (out, axis, angle in f12) the correction turn the contacts asked
#                                   for. The axis it is handed is already normalised and already
#                                   turned into the world, and its w lane is the one the unnormalised
#                                   correction had
orientation_set boot 0x001b2130 (transform, q, position) the transform written back from where the
#                              body now is
transform_touch boot 0x001b4ba0 (transform) marks it moved, so whatever hangs off it rebuilds
transform_rebuild boot 0x001b1750 (transform) the lazy rebuild of a chain of parent frames that
#                                transform_rotate and transform_unrotate begin with. Returns at once
#                                when the transform's +0x110 bit 0 is set, which is how a proof of
#                                the two of them keeps the rows it generated
list_element boot 0x0011cb80 (list, index) one element of the owner's listener list, each of which
#                           the contact distributor tells (through its own vtable +0xc, with two
#                           zero arguments) that something has touched it
# The chassis object: 0x330 bytes. What the integrate reads and writes, by offset --
#   +0x08 vtable         +0x34 speed           +0x60 centre of gravity, world
#   +0x70 acceleration   +0x80 ang accel       +0x90 euler angles
#   +0xf0/+0x100/+0x110 inverse inertia rows   +0x130 force   +0x140 torque   +0x150 impulse
#   +0x160 orientation correction (axis * angle)              +0x190 centre of gravity, local
#   +0x1a0 orientation   +0x1b4 awake          +0x1e0 contact count
#   +0x214 1/mass        +0x220 max speed^2    +0x224 max angular speed^2
#   +0x230.. per-axis angular velocity maxima  +0x240.. minima
#   +0x254 transform     +0x25c integrator     +0x260 velocity  +0x270 velocity, body frame
#   +0x280 angular velocity, world             +0x290 angular velocity, body frame
#   +0x2a0.. three linear freedom flags        +0x2ac.. three angular ones (chassis_set_flags')
#   +0x2b8/+0x2c4 the integrator's history for the position, +0x2d0/+0x2dc for the velocity,
#   +0x2f0/+0x300 for the orientation (four lanes), +0x310/+0x31c for the angular velocity

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# V5 — the motions themselves: what a clip looks like. The port is crates/game-render/src/skin.rs and
# the proofs are crates/game-render/tests/skin.rs. How each one was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-16-v5-v5-motion-clips.
#
# The chain: motion_blend_one (S6, above) walks the clip's two channel lists, samples each channel at
# phase * duration through its vtable (+0x10 rotation, +0x14 position), maps the channel's name index
# through the clip's own name->joint byte array (0xff = skip the channel), cross-fades the sample
# against the blend buffer the restart snapshotted, and writes the joint record at motion+0x234,
# stride 0x60: +0x40 the translation, +0x50 the quaternion, +0x34 the transform node they are pushed
# into. motion_blend_two does the same with both clips sampled at the same seconds.
# The rotation channel comes in two classes, one per key kind, picked by the clip's +0x2c when
# motion_channels_new allocates them. Both are 0x1c bytes with the same fields (+0 name index, +4 the
# on flag, +8 the key count, +0x14 the key array, +0x18 the cached pair) and the same vtable shape,
# whose +0x10 is the sampler and +0x20 the "reserve n keys". Kind 1 (twelve-byte keys) is the vtable at
# 0x0044dce0; kind 0 (eight-byte keys) is 0x0044dd10, and its sampler and decoder are the same code
# over bytes instead of shorts.
# Both rotation samplers have a path for a channel of fewer than three keys that weights the pair from
# the float *in front of* the key array — the allocator's own bytes. It is reachable: two rotation
# channels on the disc have two keys (Tracks\free10_ss.res :: tracks\free10_ss\anim\sh_windsock.mot).
# The port weights those from the first key instead, which is the same clip with a defined weight.
motion_rot_sample           boot 0x00136880 (seconds in f12, channel in a0, out in a1) a rotation
#                                           channel's quaternion: the cached-pair hint and its two
#                                           guesses, a forward then a backward walk, then
#                                           motion_quat_slerp_keys over the pair. Writes nothing when
#                                           the channel is off (+4) or empty. Held at the ends — and
#                                           before the first key it holds key *1*, not key 0, which
#                                           is the index its fallback uses
motion_rot_sample_narrow    boot 0x00136de0 (seconds in f12, channel in a0, out in a1) the same
#                                           sampler for the eight-byte key: the same hint, the same
#                                           two walks, the same held ends and the same key-1 fallback,
#                                           with a stride of 8 and motion_quat_slerp_keys_narrow
motion_pos_sample           boot 0x00137e70 (seconds in f12, channel in a0, out in a1) a position
#                                           channel's translation, w = 1. Past the last key it
#                                           extrapolates along the last interval rather than holding.
#                                           Its other path — channel off, or fewer than two keys —
#                                           writes the key's z into x and never writes z at all; no
#                                           clip on this disc has fewer than six keys in a channel
motion_pos_bracket          boot 0x001380b0 (seconds in f12, keys in a0, count in a1, out a/b/u) the
#                                           binary search the position sampler uses: -> false and the
#                                           last two keys when the time is at or past the last, and
#                                           with a time before the first key it brackets keys[-1]
motion_quat_decode          boot 0x00100a50 (key in a0, out in a1) one 8-byte rotation key as a
#                                           quaternion: axis * sin/32767 with the cosine's sign folded
#                                           in, w = |cos|, so w is never negative
motion_quat_decode_narrow   boot 0x001008e0 (key in a0, out in a1) the four-byte rotation: a u8 turn
#                                           index into motion_turn_table_narrow and three i8 axis
#                                           components, otherwise identical to motion_quat_decode
motion_quat_slerp_keys      boot 0x0013f000 (weight in f12, key a in a1, key b in a2, out in a0)
#                                           decodes both keys and hands them to motion_slerp with the
#                                           shortest-path flag set, which is the only caller that sets
#                                           it
motion_quat_slerp_keys_narrow boot 0x0013ef20 (weight in f12, key a in a1, key b in a2, out in a0) the
#                                           same for two four-byte keys
transform_set_rotation      boot 0x001b2130 (transform in a0, quaternion in a1, 0 in a2) writes the
#                                           quaternion as three rows at +0x40/+0x50/+0x60 — the
#                                           row-vector form, the transpose of the textbook one — then
#                                           marks the chain dirty. a2 non-zero loads the identity
#                                           instead
transform_dirty             boot 0x001b1930 (transform) the "this frame's matrix is stale" mark
#                                           transform_set_rotation ends in; hooked in the proofs
# 0x001b3060 (out, transform, p) -> p turned into the transform's frame without its origin; the
#   mirroring path's half of transform_unpoint. Named `orientation_transform_point` above — V5 found
#   it independently and called it `transform_rotate`, but S19 gave that name to 0x001b2ff0, a
#   different function, and its tests resolve it. Use the established name; nothing in V5 resolves
#   this one.
#
# The two tables the quaternion code reads. Both are built at start-up into memory nothing in the
# image's call graph writes, so the port rebuilds them from sin/cos/acos; a save state's copies agree
# to 1.8e-7 and 1.1e-5. Addresses, not functions.
motion_turn_table           boot 0x0045fa20 4096 entries of (f32 sin(n*pi/4096)/32767, f32
#                                           cos(n*pi/4096)), stride 8. The cosine's sign bit is the
#                                           one motion_quat_decode folds into the axis
motion_turn_table_narrow    boot 0x00467a20 256 entries of (f32 sin(n*pi/256)/127, f32 cos(n*pi/256)),
#                                           stride 8: the same table for the four-byte key, and it
#                                           sits immediately after the wide one (0x45fa20 + 0x8000)
motion_arc_table            boot 0x0046a688 a pointer to 30002 f32 of acos(i/14999.5 - 1) — 0x0062eaa0
#                                           in the save state. The last two live entries are
#                                           0.00048828125, not an arc cosine of anything, and the
#                                           slerp reads them for any dot product above 0.99997
motion_arc_scale            boot 0x0046a6a0 14999.5: the f32 a dot product's magnitude is scaled by,
#                                           and also the table's own middle
motion_sine_poly            boot 0x00426e10 the slerp's sine series: 1, -1/6, 1/120, -1/5040
motion_slerp_one            boot 0x0041f730 1.0: the dot product at or past which the slerp returns
#                                           its `from` untouched instead of dividing by sin 0
#
# The clip on disc. All 17,337 `.mot` members in the disc's archives are version 3 with position key
# kind 0 (sixteen bytes); 17,295 are rotation key kind 1 (twelve bytes) and 42 are rotation key kind 0
# (eight) — the trucks and scrapers of the free-ride tracks, e.g. Tracks\free06_ss.res ::
# tracks\free06_ss\anim\scraper_01.mot. The 40-byte position key and versions 1 and 2 are read by
# motion_clip_read but appear nowhere on the disc.
motion_clip_read            boot 0x00142750 (archive in a1, clip in a2) the `.mot` reader: the version
#                                           and frame count, the name table, the duration (+0x3c), the
#                                           two key kinds (+0x2c rotation, +0x30 position), the two
#                                           channel counts (+0x20 position, +0x24 rotation), then the
#                                           channels — count into chan+8, name index into chan+0, then
#                                           the keys read raw into chan+0x14
motion_channels_new         boot 0x00140c80 (clip, count) the rotation channel array: an array of
#                                           `count` 0x1c-byte channels of the class the clip's
#                                           rotation kind names, and a list of pointers to them at
#                                           clip+0x1c
motion_rot_channel_new      boot 0x00135cd0 (chan) a rotation channel: +4 = 1 on, +8 = 0 keys,
#                                           +0x14 = no key array, +0x18 = 1, the cached pair
motion_rot_channel_new_narrow boot 0x00135c00 (chan) the same for the eight-byte key kind, with the
#                                           other vtable
motion_pos_channel_new      boot 0x00135900 (chan) a position channel: the same without the cache
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S18: the .phy reader. bike_phy_load (above) opens the bike's file through these ten, and every
# parameter in the game comes off the disc through them.
ini_new boot 0x00149f20 (reader) -> the empty reader: a 0x18b0-byte object on the caller's stack
ini_open boot 0x0014a3e0 (reader, name, 0, -1, in_memory) -> 1 if it has the file. The fifth argument
#                        is a {u64 length, bytes...} block for a file somebody else already loaded;
#                        with 0 there it opens `name` through fs_open/fs_size/fs_read/fs_close.
#                        Whitespace is removed rather than trimmed, except inside [] or quotes;
#                        quotes are removed; a line whose squeezed form starts with ';' is a comment;
#                        the value is everything after the first '='; a pair with an empty value is
#                        dropped. Matching is case-insensitive.
ini_del boot 0x00149f80 (reader, -1) -> frees it
ini_section boot 0x0014a7a0 (reader, name) -> 1 if the section is there, and it becomes the one the
#                           getters read. A second section with a name already seen is a second
#                           section: the getters find the first one's keys.
ini_text boot 0x0014af90 (reader, key, dst, size) -> 1 and the value copied, or 0 and dst untouched
ini_int boot 0x0014b3f0 (reader, key, dst) -> T/F/ON/OFF/Y/N first, else strtol with base 0
ini_float boot 0x0014b520 (reader, key, dst) -> strtod, then double_to_float, which truncates
ini_bool boot 0x0014b6f0 (default, reader, key, dst) -> like ini_int but with a default
ini_float_default boot 0x0014b790 (default in f12, reader, key, dst) -> always writes dst
ini_quadword boot 0x0014b810 (reader, key, default, dst) -> up to four floats, split at any of
#                            " \t,;" then "();" then "()," ; lanes the text does not reach keep the
#                            default's. The defaults the bike's loader passes: 0x00426d70 (0,0,0,1)
#                            and 0x00426d90 (-1,-1,-1,1), which the physics reads as "unset".
# The two pieces of ini_open worth naming on their own: 0x0014c300 classifies one squeezed line
# (comment, section, pair) and 0x0014c8c0 takes the pair apart at the first '='.
strtod boot 0x0040ac20 (text, end) -> the double a decimal string names; "0x" hex floats, inf and nan
#                      are all accepted, and the port's reader does not model them (no retail file
#                      holds one)
strtol boot 0x00412e38 (text, end, base) -> base 0 reads 0x hex and leading-0 octal
strtok boot 0x00412b18 (text, delimiters) -> the C one, with the static cursor
fs_open boot 0x001255f0 (mode, name) -> a handle, or -1
fs_size boot 0x00125d00 (handle) -> the file's length in bytes
fs_read boot 0x001246f0 (file, handle, dst, length) -> bytes read
fs_close boot 0x00125400 (handle)
rigid_body_set_mass boot 0x00158dc0 (mass in f12, body) -> the chassis' vtable slot +0x78, which is
#                            how the loader hands over Weight/32.2
# The seven the loader reaches a wheel through, all taking the wheel as the first integer argument
# while the floats come in f12 up (the EE ABI fills the two banks independently):
wheel_set_suspension boot 0x0025ac50 (strut, max compression rate, max decompression rate, spring,
#                                    bounce ratio, rebound ratio in f12..f17, wheel) -> and the rate
#                                    it works out lands at wheel+0x54, which the loader reads back
wheel_set_tire boot 0x0025acd0 (radius, inertia, 6.0, 24.0 in f12..f15, wheel)
wheel_set_surface_drive boot 0x0025ada0 (SideForce, SlippingSideForce, MaxDriveForce, MaxSideForce,
#                                       MaxSlippingSideForce, SkidDriveForce, SkidSideForce,
#                                       SkidSlippingSideForce in f12..f19, wheel, surface)
wheel_set_surface_side boot 0x0025ade0 (SlippingSideForceRoundOff/DropOff/Scale/SpeedScale,
#                                      BrakingSideForceRoundOff/DropOff/Scale/SpeedScale in f12..f19,
#                                      wheel, surface, then SideForceRoundOff/DropOff/Scale/
#                                      SpeedScale and DonutsSideForceSpeedScale on the stack, a slot
#                                      every eight bytes: sp+0x0, +0x8, +0x10, +0x18, +0x20.
#                                      [Front Tire] has no DonutsSideForceSpeedScale%d key, and the
#                                      front's call stores a zero in that slot (sw $zero, 0x20($sp))
wheel_set_surface_drag boot 0x0025ae50 (MaxBrakingTorque, WheelFriction, WheelDrag in f12..f14,
#                                      wheel, surface)
wheel_set_steering boot 0x0025ae80 (damping, inertia, torque in f12..f14, wheel) -- the front only
wheel_set_tire_derived boot 0x0025b160 (wheel) -> the numbers the wheel works out for itself once its
#                                      tire and surfaces are in
bike_physics_lines boot 0x00253900 (bike) -> called between the tires and the engine; does nothing
#                                  unless bike+0x7c8 is set
# The seven the collision record is set through, the record being bike+0x7d0:
collision_set_mass boot 0x0026fd80 (CollisionWeight * 0.0310559 in f12, record, CofG offset)
collision_set_springs boot 0x0026ff10 (MaxCompression, Spring, Damping, Restitution, Body* of the
#                                     same four in f12..f19, record)
collision_set_high_speed boot 0x00270220 (ray speed scale, unpenetration distance, cos of the minimum
#                                        angle in f12..f14, record, enabled, ray offset)
collision_set_angular boot 0x002701a0 (record, enabled, body enabled)
collision_set_moment_scales boot 0x002700d0 (record, spring, friction, body spring, body friction)
collision_set_friction boot 0x002701d0 (Friction, BodyFriction in f12..f13, record)
collision_set_surface_friction boot 0x002701b0 (TerrainFriction in f12, record, surface) -> called for
#                                              all sixteen surfaces whatever NumberOfSurfaces says
# The C library's one lock, which strtod's helper at 0x0040ac80 takes and gives back. An oracle has
# one thread, so both halves are hooked to nothing — otherwise the first number read reaches the EE
# kernel's GetThreadId (syscall 47, 0x003fbed0) and WaitSema.
libc_lock boot 0x0040e250 () -> GetThreadId, and WaitSema(0x00422b10) if the owner at 0x004241e0 is
#                   somebody else; the count at 0x004241e4 makes it recursive
libc_unlock boot 0x0040e2d0 () -> the other half
sbrk boot 0x00410560 (reent, increment) -> the break before the increment, which is how the C
#                    library's own malloc — the one strtod's hard rounding path allocates from —
#                    grows. It insists the blocks be contiguous, so a hook has to bump a pointer

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S20 — the animation caller: the clip table each rider state asks a clip out of, the one function in
# the game that starts a clip-to-clip transition, the pose snapshot a cross-fade fades from, and the
# fourth arm of the library dispatch. The port is the same crates/game-sim/src/motion.rs the S6 block
# above describes, the proofs are crates/game-sim/tests/motion.rs, and how each one was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-16-s20-s20-animation-caller.
#
# Two corrections to the S6 block. motion_blend_three takes *four* clip handles, not three — three
# weights over two pairs: a/b mixed by f13, c/d mixed by the same f13, the two results mixed by f14.
# And +0x4c of the motion object is not "which transition sampler": it is the clip handle a running
# transition is coming out of, written by motion_play below. Its *nullness* picks the sampler, because
# a transition out of a real clip samples that clip at the fade clock (+0x88) while one out of nothing
# samples the pose snapshot instead -- both of them weighted by the same smooth step.
#
# The dispatch itself is at the tail of stunt_clocks: `./kit dis 0x259b00 0x259c00`. The mode lives in
# $17 and the whole function writes it exactly four times -- 0 in the prologue, 2 in the airborne arm,
# 1 in the two arms that end on the neutral pose -- so the rider reaches modes 0, 1 and 2 and nothing
# else. $21, $22 and $23, the arguments past the handles, are zeroed in the prologue and never written
# again, which is why Ghidra prints them as literal zeros. Mode 3 is not a case in either copy of the
# dispatch: a 3 falls out of the bottom and the frame makes no call at all.
vehicle_animation boot 0x002492b0 the vehicle's own animation step, which is the second copy of the
#                                same dispatch (`./kit dis 0x249b20 0x249c40`) and has a sixth arm at
#                                mode 5. It is the caller for the four-clip blend: it writes 4 into
#                                $17 at 0x002497b4 and 0x002498d4, two arms of a 60-entry jump table
#                                (0x00431990, indexed by its own animation state at +0x8f0) that each
#                                pick two pairs
#                                of clips out of its own tables at +0x960..+0x980 and hand them over
#                                with the within-pair mix in f13 and the between-pair mix in f14.
#                                Ghidra drops both of those blocks. Not ported: it is a different
#                                object's animation, and only the shape of the call it makes is here
motion_play boot 0x001464a0 (length in f12, motion in a0, clip handle in a1, keep the phase in a2)
#                          put a clip on. The only writer of +0x80 anywhere, so every clip-to-clip
#                          transition in the game starts here -- and nothing in the rider's animation
#                          section calls it. Its four callers are the wrapper below, the vehicle's
#                          animation step at 0x002496e4 (fade length from its own +0x690, the clip out
#                          of its table at +0x900, phase not kept, and mode 0 after), and two other
#                          actors at 0x0032b138 and 0x0032ea40. length 0 is a hard cut: +0x3c = 0,
#                          +0x48 = the clip, +0x80 = 0, +0x84 = 0. Otherwise the transition: +0x90 and
#                          +0x88 = the old clip time, +0x8c = that plus length, +0x94 = length,
#                          +0x4c = the old clip -- and if there was no old clip, or a transition was
#                          already running, +0x4c = 0 and anim_rewind takes the pose snapshot instead.
#                          Then +0x48 = the clip, +0x84 = 0, +0x80 = 1. With a2 set, the clip time is
#                          carried across as new.duration * (old time / old.duration): computed from
#                          the old clip *before* any of this and written over +0x84 after all of it
motion_play_clip boot 0x00146490 (motion in a0, clip handle in a1, keep the phase in a2) a tail jump
#                               into motion_play with the length taken from the clip's own +0x40, so
#                               a clip carries the time it wants to be faded in over
anim_joint_rotation boot 0x001b25f0 (joint node in a0, out in a1) the node's local rotation as a
#                                  quaternion, through mat4_to_quat on its matrix at +0x40
anim_joint_translation boot 0x001b2840 (joint node in a0, out in a1) the node's local translation at
#                                     +0x70, with 1.0 written into the fourth lane
anim_fill_words boot 0x00148a70 (motion in a0, out pointer in a1, value in a2) allocate one word per
#                              joint and fill it with the value. The snapshot's tag buffer
anim_clip_handle boot 0x00146370 (motion in a0, clip in a1) the handle this motion object has bound
#                               for that clip, by searching its list (+0x40 count, +0x44 an array of
#                               pointers to handles) for the handle whose +4 is the clip. 0 when it
#                               has none -- which is what an unbound clip and a missing clip both
#                               come out as, so the port cannot tell those two apart either
anim_clip_by_name boot 0x001426a0 (set in a0, name in a1) the set's clip of that name: +0xc the
#                                count, +0x10 an array of pointers to clips, each clip's first word a
#                                pointer to its name, compared with stricmp -- so clip names are
#                                matched without case. The third argument is unused
anim_set boot 0x00479978 not code: the word holding the one animation set the game loads clips out
#                        of. Every lookup in rider_load_clips goes through it, so a proof points it at
#                        a fabricated set
anim_find_or_load boot 0x00142430 (0 in f12, set in a0, motion in a1, name in a2, flag in a3) the
#                                clip of that name, loaded off the disc if the set has not got it.
#                                Reads files, so a proof has to hook it
anim_bind_clip boot 0x001445b0 (motion in a0, clip in a1) bind a clip to this motion object: the
#                             8-byte handle {joint map, clip}, the joint map built by matching the
#                             clip's channel names against the skeleton (0xff for a channel with no
#                             joint), the clip's reference count bumped, and the handle appended to
#                             the motion object's array. Allocates three ways
rider_load_clips boot 0x00258790 the same function the S6 block names stunt_specials, which does more
#                               than find the two special slots. Two passes over the 24 records, each
#                               building "<prefix>bike_<Name>" with snprintf into a 32-byte buffer:
#                               the first calls anim_find_or_load and anim_bind_clip for every record
#                               whose UsedForBike (+0x4c) is set, the second writes
#                               anim_clip_handle's answer into +0xa88 + id*4 (and a plain 0 when the
#                               set has no such clip). Then "bike_air_Fwd", "bike_air_Mid" and
#                               "bike_air_Bck" straight into +0xaec, +0xaf0 and +0xaf4 -- named
#                               fields that happen to sit where ids 25, 26 and 27 would index, with
#                               the id-24 word at +0xae8 never written. The strings are at 0x004336d0
#                               ("%sbike_%s") and 0x00433700..0x00433720
#   the pose snapshot's buffers, on the motion object: +0x230 the joint count, +0x234 the joint array
#   (stride 0x60, the node at +0x34), +0x248 one quaternion per joint, +0x24c one translation per
#   joint, +0x250 the tag buffer and +0x254 whether it has one. All five call sites of anim_rewind
#   pass a null tag array, so the tag path is dead code in this image

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# S17 — the rider's four controllers. The three the ground step drives every substep and the
# no-hander it drives when the bike is not balancing, all four of them listed but not ported by S3.
# The port is crates/game-sim/src/move.rs (stand_upright, balance, roll_pitch, no_hander) and the
# proofs are in crates/game-sim/tests/move.rs; how each one was found is in
# research/journal/2026-10-16-s17-the-rider-controllers. The four addresses are in the S3 block above.
#
# All four are read through `./kit dis`, not the decompiler: every one of them builds its accumulator
# chain in an order Ghidra reorders, and one of them calls a function with seven register arguments,
# which Ghidra prints as a call with two.
#
# bike_stand_upright 0x002574e0 — the low-speed upright controller. Only with both wheels down, not
#   balancing, no throttle and |speed| < 10 (a literal, not a key). It takes the part of gravity that
#   runs along the ground — gravity less its component along the front wheel's contact normal — splits
#   that into the part along the chassis' forward axis flattened into the contact plane and the part
#   across it, and pushes back: minus the across part always, minus the along part as well while the
#   brake is held. The forward axis comes from orientation_axes against the chassis' own transform,
#   which may be null; the function does not check, and the null answer is (0,0,1,0). A flattened
#   forward of exactly zero length makes it give up and add nothing.
# bike_balance 0x002561f0 — balance on one wheel. Only with the front wheel in contact. Four
#   accumulators, all of them at +0xa4c..+0xa6f and all of them zeroed by the step whenever the bike
#   is not balancing (S3 read that clearing as the no-hander's; it is this one's):
#     +0xa4c balance_inertia      the rate the rider's input is let through, ramped by
#                                 BalanceInertiaRate between MinBalanceInertia and MaxBalanceInertia
#     +0xa60 balance_command      the command the stick and the noise make, clamped to +-1
#     +0xa64 balance_point        the lean the command integrates to, clamped to +-MaxBalancePoint
#     +0xa6c balance_noise_time   the noise's own clock
#   The roll angle it controls to comes from the front wheel's contact normal (front+0x130) brought
#   into the bike node's frame, flattened into the frame's xy plane, normalised, and read off the acos
#   table — the same lookup air.rs::AcosTable::at already is. The controller is a P term on that angle
#   less the roll rate (chassis+0x290's z) and less the disturbance the balance point commands, and
#   what it moves is the chassis' centre of gravity in x, clamped to +-RollControlMaxCG.
# bike_roll_pitch 0x00256470 — the roll and pitch the rider's lean feeds, on the ground only. Two
#   halves, each a PI controller on a pitch rate with the integral at +0x724:
#     the wheelie — rear wheel down, throttle on, speed over 5 (a literal), the *rear* brake off
#       (+0x654), no clutch, and the stick pulled back past 0.5. The command fades out with speed
#       through +0x720's 1/WheelieTopSpeed.
#     the stoppie — latched at +0x728 by the *front* brake (+0x650) and the stick pushed forward past
#       -1, and dropped
#       again below -0.1 of stick or under 7 of speed. It adds a yaw term as well:
#       1000 * (StoppieYawRateScale * lean - the rate about the rear contact normal).
#   The wheelie half measures the same rate against the same normal and multiplies the result by a
#   literal zero, so that whole dot product is dead.
# bike_no_hander 0x00255d20 — rolls the bike node about its own forward axis so the rider can let go.
#   The angle it rolls towards is the ground's roll angle under whichever wheels are properly down
#   (a ray hit, or a swept contact whose normal is more than 0.17 up), faded out with speed, with the
#   rider's lean commanding a roll up to MaxRollCommand — widened by DonutRollAngleScale while the
#   donut is on — and RollGain * dt of first-order lag on the way. It ends in transform_set_axes.
transform_set_axes boot 0x001b1e20 (node, relative_to, forward, up, sync_a in t0, sync_b in t1,
#                                  sync_c in t2) -> builds the node's world matrix from two axes by
#                                  Gram-Schmidt: row0 (+0x40) = forward x up, row1 (+0x90) = up,
#                                  row2 (+0xa0) = forward, row3 the translation it keeps. The pair
#                                  orientation_axes reads back out, which is how a2 is known to be the
#                                  forward (z) axis and a3 the up (y) axis. Ghidra prints the
#                                  orthonormalising callee (0x0013e330) as a two-argument call; it
#                                  takes five, in a2, a3, t0, t1 and t2, passed straight through
# P32: transform_set_axes' one callee is mat4_basis (0x0013e330, the port's math::basis, proved), at
#   the node's own translation (+0x70..+0x78), with orthogonalise = t0 = 1 and keep_forward = t1 = 1
#   (./kit dis 0x001b1e20). So the "Gram-Schmidt" above is mat4_basis, not a cross of its own; the
#   port's ride.rs set_axes is now that call.
# P32: the node bike_no_hander writes is *(bike+0x18), which is the chassis' transform +0x254 (see
#   bike_attach_nodes below), and chassis_integrate (0x0015c540) begins by turning that transform's
#   rotation into a quaternion and folding the change since its own +0x1a0 into the body. So the
#   no-hander's roll is the body's roll: the lean a grounded bike shows is the chassis', although
#   ang_free pins its roll *rate*. Checked in recording2's save-state RAM: for all four bikes
#   *(bike+0x18) == *(chassis+0x254), and that transform's rows (at +0x80..+0xb0) equal the rows of
#   the chassis quaternion at +0x1a0. Keys there: RollGain 5.0, MaxRollCommand 0.7854,
#   MaxRollSpeed 51.333, MinStandUprightSpeed 14.667, MaxStandUprightSpeed 44.0.
# The two brake words the two halves read are not copies of one another: the bike's setup at
# 0x002535a0 hands the front wheel a pointer to bike+0x650 as its +0x30 and the rear wheel one to
# bike+0x654, beside the engine's +0x658/+0x65c (the rate-limited throttle and clutch), and each wheel
# reads its own word as a brake — 0x0025c4c8 in wheel_surface_drag turns it into a torque against the
# spin, 0x0025cb8c in wheel_tire_forces gates the locked-wheel path on it being nonzero. So the
# wheelie's veto is the driven wheel's brake and the stoppie's gate is the front's. Who writes the two
# words (one lever into two rate limiters, or two levers) was not chased down; the port keeps them as
# two inputs, Step::front_brake and Step::rear_brake.
# rotate_about_axis 0x001344e0 (the S7 block has it: angle in f12, unit axis, v, out) is the
# no-hander's last arithmetic — the ground normal turned about the node's own forward axis by minus
# the angle. Ported and proved by S7 as camera::rotate.
# The .phy keys the four controllers read, on top of S3's table. The loader is bike_phy_load
# 0x002539e0; a default in brackets is the one the loader passes when the key is absent.
#   DonutRollAngleScale       +0x540  [1.0]  (loaded at 0x00255380, far down the same function)
#   MaxRollCommand            +0x6d8  * 0.01745329 (degrees)
#   MaxRollSpeed              +0x6e0  * 1.46666
#   RollGain                  +0x6e8
#   MinStandUprightSpeed      +0x6ec  [10.0] * 1.46666
#   MaxStandUprightSpeed      +0x6f0  [30.0] * 1.46666
#   RollControlGain           +0x704
#   RollControlRateGain       +0x708
#   RollControlMaxCG          +0x70c
#   MaxRollRateDisturbance    +0x710
#   MaxPitchCommand           +0x714  * 0.01745329
#   PitchGain                 +0x718
#   PitchRateGain             +0x71c
#   WheelieTopSpeed           +0x720  stored as 1.0 / (key * 1.46666)
#   MaxStoppiePitchCommand    +0x730  * 0.01745329
